Abstract
We establish ring isomorphisms between quantum Grothendieck rings of certain remarkable monoidal categories 
C
Q
,
B
n
 and 
C
Q
,
A
2
n
−
1
 of finite-dimensional representations of quantum affine algebras of types 
B
n
(
1
)
 and 
A
2
n
−
1
(
1
)
, respectively. Our proof relies in part on the corresponding quantum cluster algebra structures. Moreover, we prove that our isomorphisms specialize at 
t
=
1
 to the isomorphisms of (classical) Grothendieck rings obtained recently by Kashiwara, Kim and Oh by other methods. As a consequence, we prove a conjecture formulated by the first author in 2002: the multiplicities of simple modules in standard modules in 
C
Q
,
B
n
 are given by the specialization of certain analogues of Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials and the coefficients of these polynomials are positive.
Keywords
Quantum affine algebras; Quantum Grothendieck rings; Kazhdan-Lusztig algorithm; T-systems; Quantum cluster algebras; Dual canonical bases

1. Introduction
Cluster algebras can be realized in terms of various important monoidal categories, such as finite-dimensional representations of quantum affine algebras, perverse sheaves on quiver varieties, representations of quiver-Hecke algebras, equivariant perverse coherent sheaves on the affine Grassmannian... (see the very recent [6] and [28] for a review).
In this paper, inspired by this general framework of monoidal categorifications, we get new results on the representation theory of quantum affine algebras and on the positivity of certain corresponding Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials.
Let 
U
q
(
g
ˆ
)
 be an untwisted quantum affine algebra of quantum parameter 
q
∈
C
×
 which is not a root of unity. Consider 
C
 to be the monoidal category of finite-dimensional representations of the algebra 
U
q
(
g
ˆ
)
 (those representations are also regarded as representations of a quantum loop algebra 
U
q
(
L
g
)
). This category has been studied from several geometric, algebraic, combinatorial perspectives in connections to various fields, see [51], [38], [17] for important recent developments and [11] for a very recent point of view in the context of physics.
The category 
C
 is non-semisimple, and non-braided. It has a very intricate structure and many basic questions are still open. The simple objects in 
C
 have been classified by Chari-Pressley in terms of Drinfeld polynomials [8], but the dimensions and characters of these simple objects are not known in general.
The fundamental modules in 
C
 are distinguished simple modules whose classes generate its Grothendieck ring 
K
(
C
)
. (We do not distinguish the term “module” from “representation” in this paper.) For simply-laced types, Nakajima established in [54] a remarkable Kazhdan-Lusztig algorithm to compute the multiplicity 
P
m
,
m
′
 of a simple module 
L
(
m
′
)
 in a standard module 
M
(
m
)
, that is, a tensor product of fundamental modules. Here m, 
m
′
 belong to an ordered set of monomials 
M
 which parametrizes both simple and standard objects. This allows one to calculate the classes of simple modules 
[
L
(
m
)
]
∈
K
(
C
)
 in terms of the classes of fundamental modules. As the dimensions and characters of the latter are known, this gives an answer to the problem of calculating the dimensions and characters of arbitrary simple objects: the multiplicities 
P
m
,
m
′
 are proved to be the evaluation at 
t
=
1
 of analogues 
P
m
,
m
′
(
t
)
 of Kazhdan-Lusztig polynomials. These polynomials are constructed from the structure of the quantum Grothendieck ring 
K
t
(
C
)
, which is a t-deformation of 
K
(
C
)
 in a certain quantum torus [54], [60]. Besides it is proved that the coefficients of the polynomials 
P
m
,
m
′
(
t
)
 are positive, as well as the structure constants of the quantum Grothendieck ring 
K
t
(
C
)
 on a canonical basis whose elements are called 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters of simple modules. These results are based on the geometric construction of standard modules in terms of quiver varieties known only for simply-laced types (note however that geometric character formulas for standard modules have been obtained in [31] for all types).
The existence of an algorithm to compute the multiplicity of a simple module in a standard module is still not known in the case of non-simply-laced untwisted quantum affine algebras. A conjectural answer was proposed by the first author in [23] by giving another construction of the quantum Grothendieck ring 
K
t
(
C
)
 and the corresponding polynomials 
P
m
,
m
′
(
t
)
 which can be extended to general types. This leads to a general precise conjectural formula for the multiplicity of simple modules in standard modules
(1.1)
[
M
(
m
)
]
=
[
L
(
m
)
]
+
∑
m
′
<
m
P
m
,
m
′
(
1
)
[
L
(
m
′
)
]
,
where < is the partial ordering on monomials as above. Besides it is not clear from [23] that the polynomials 
P
m
,
m
′
(
t
)
 have positive coefficients (although it has been checked in numerous cases by computer). As far as the authors know, these two independent conjectures, (1.1) and positivity, are completely open at the moment. In the present paper, we establish these conjectures for certain remarkable monoidal categories in the case of type 
B
n
(
1
)
.
For a simply-laced type 
X
n
(
1
)
, the category 
C
 has interesting monoidal subcategories 
C
Q
,
X
n
 introduced in [30] depending on a quiver 
Q
 whose underlying graph is the Dynkin diagram of type 
X
n
. This monoidal subcategory 
C
Q
,
X
n
 is sometimes called a Hernandez-Leclerc subcategory. It is proved in [30] that the quantum Grothendieck ring 
K
t
(
C
Q
,
X
n
)
 is isomorphic to the quantized coordinate algebra 
A
t
−
1
[
N
−
X
n
]
 of type 
X
n
 (it was recently established [16] that certain completions of these categories have a structure of affine highest weight category). For type 
B
n
(
1
)
, analogues 
C
Q
,
B
n
 of the categories 
C
Q
,
X
n
 were defined in [57]. In this paper, we establish an analogue of the isomorphism in [30] for 
C
Q
,
B
n
:
(1.2)
K
t
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
≃
A
t
−
1
[
N
−
A
2
n
−
1
]
(Theorem 10.1, Corollary 10.2). One notable point about this isomorphism is that the quantized coordinate algebra side is of type 
A
2
n
−
1
, and not of type 
B
n
.
In the proof of (1.2), we use the quantum cluster structure of the quantized coordinate algebras as follows. We first show the isomorphism between ambient quantum tori of 
K
t
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
 and 
A
t
−
1
[
N
−
A
2
n
−
1
]
. The former arises from the definition of (truncated) 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters and the latter comes from the quantum cluster algebra structure of 
A
t
−
1
[
N
−
A
2
n
−
1
]
. Then we prove the correspondence between distinguished systems of relations: the quantum T-system of type B that we establish in 
K
t
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
 (Theorem 9.6) and some specific mutation sequence in the quantum cluster algebra structure. This strategy is partially in line with the one in [30] aside from some technical differences. For example, we do not know a priori some positivity properties of (truncated) 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters for type 
B
n
(
1
)
.
As an immediate corollary of (1.2), we can say that 
K
t
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
 has a quantum cluster algebra structure coming from that of 
A
t
−
1
[
N
−
A
2
n
−
1
]
. Then the initial quiver for this quantum cluster algebra structure is nothing but a subquiver of the quiver introduced by the first author and Leclerc [31] which is defined for a subcategory of the category 
C
 of type 
B
n
(
1
)
. See Remark 6.2, Remark 10.3.
We also show that the isomorphism (1.2) maps a basis 
{
L
t
(
m
)
}
m
 of 
K
t
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
 parametrized by simple modules 
L
(
m
)
 in 
C
Q
,
B
n
 (called the 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters of the simple modules 
L
(
m
)
 as above) to the dual canonical basis of the quantized coordinate algebra in the sense of Lusztig and Kashiwara. This implies the positivity property conjectured in [23] for the category 
C
Q
,
B
n
:
(P1)
The positivity of the coefficients of the polynomials 
P
m
,
m
′
(
t
)
.
As a by-product we also establish two additional positivity properties:
(P2)
The positivity of structure constants of the quantum Grothendieck ring 
K
t
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
.
(P3)
The positivity of the coefficients of the truncated 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters of 
L
(
m
)
.
As mentioned before, the following isomorphism was proved in [30]:
(1.3)
K
t
(
C
Q
,
A
2
n
−
1
)
≃
A
t
−
1
[

−

−

]
.
Combining (1.3) with our isomorphism (1.2), we obtain an isomorphism between quantum Grothendieck rings
(1.4)
K
t
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
≃
K
t
(
C
Q
,
A
2
n
−
1
)
.
Here we should note that 
C
Q
,
B
n
 is determined by the Dynkin quiver 
Q
′
 of type 
A
2
n
−
2
, which is irrelevant to the 
A
2
n
−
1
-quiver 
Q
, and some additional data 
♭
∈
{
>
,
<
}
. See section 3 for details (the category 
C
Q
,
B
n
 above is denoted by 
C
(
Q
′
)
♭
 there). Since the isomorphism (1.3) also restricts to a bijection between the basis consisting of the 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters of simple modules and the dual canonical basis, the isomorphism (1.4) induces a bijection between the 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters of simple modules in 
C
Q
,

n
 and 
C
Q
,

2
n
−

. See also Remark 12.3.
We note that similarities between finite-dimensional modules of type A and B quantum affine algebras had been observed in [26]. The Kirillov-Reshetikhin modules are certain simple objects in 
C
 generalizing fundamental modules. It is proved they are thin in types A and B, that is, they have multiplicity-free Frenkel-Reshetikhin q-characters [15], and that these q-characters have analogous explicit formulas in both types (certain 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters of Kirillov-Reshetikhin modules are used in the construction of 
K
t
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
).
To prove that the elements 
{
L
t
(
m
)
}
m
 specialize to the classes of simple modules as 
t
→
1
, and to establish the formula (1.1) in 
C
Q
,
B
n
, we combine our results with those of Kashiwara, Kim and Oh [43], [42]. These authors used different tools as in the present paper, namely generalized quantum affine Schur-Weyl duality [33], to construct an isomorphism 
K
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
≃
K
(
C
Q
,
A
2
n
−
1
)
 between classical Grothendieck rings. Moreover they proved their isomorphism preserves the basis of simple modules. We show that our isomorphism (1.4) of quantum Grothendieck rings specializes at 
t
=
1
 to their isomorphism (note that, as far as the authors know, the isomorphism (1.4) can not be deduced directly from the results of the papers [43], [42]). This coincidence implies for the category 
C
Q
,
B
n
 that the 
(
q
,
t
)
-character 
L
t
(
m
)
 specializes to the q-character of 
L
(
m
)
 as conjectured by the first author in [23]. In particular the conjectural formula (1.1) holds for this category.1
We obtain also the explicit correspondence of 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters of simple modules under (1.4) for special quivers. This is computed from the formulas for the change of the Lusztig parametrizations of the dual canonical basis associated with a change of reduced words. We observe that (1.4) does not preserve any of the classes of modules one might naturally expect (e.g. fundamental or Kirillov-Reshetikhin modules). See Example 12.11, Example 12.12. When the authors started to work on this correspondence, the paper [42] of Kashiwara, Kim and Oh appeared, and they also compute such correspondence in a different way. Our correspondence from type 
A
2
n
−
1
(
1
)
 to type 
B
n
(
1
)
 coincides with the correspondence in [42, section 3] up to shift of spectral parameters when we restrict it to 
C
Q
,
A
2
n
−
1
. We note that we also describe the correspondence from type 
B
n
(
1
)
 to type 
A
2
n
−
1
(
1
)
 in this paper, which seems not to be written in [43] and [42].
Some parts of the proof in this paper, in particular for the quantum T-systems, rely on the thinness of Kirillov-Reshetikhin modules discussed above. Although this property is not satisfied in general, it is satisfied by fundamental modules in types C and 
G
2
 [26], which gives hope to get analogous results in these situations. We plan to come back to this in another paper.
We expect that we could also study the whole category 
C
 of type 
B
n
(
1
)
 by “gluing” (infinitely many) copies of 
C
Q
,
B
n
 together (cf. [30, subsection 1.5, sections 7, 8]). For example, we hope that we could describe a structure of 
K
t
(
C
)
 by revealing how the copies of 
K
t
(
C
Q
,
B
n
)
 are “patched” in 
K
t
(
C
)
. In simply-laced cases, a “patching” of 
K
t
(
C
Q
,
X
n
)
 was calculated in a uniform manner in [30] by means of derived Hall algebras, whose analogues for non-simply-laced cases are not known now. We also plan to discuss the whole category 
C
 in a future paper.
Moreover, the meaning of the quantum Grothendieck ring 
K
t
(
C
)
 in terms of the structure of the category 
C
 has not been clarified in non-simply-laced cases yet. Hence it is not known at the moment whether the isomorphism (1.4) arises as an equivalence of categories, but it is certainly a question we would like to address.
The paper is organized as follows. In section 2, we briefly recall the definitions of quantum loop algebras and their finite-dimensional representation theory. In section 3, we explain the definition of the remarkable monoidal categories considered in this paper following [30] and [57]. Here we also show the important convexity of twisted Auslander-Reiten quivers (Lemma 3.22). In section 4, we recall the definition of quantum tori which will be ambient spaces of 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters. The important data are the inverses of quantum Cartan matrices. Their explicit form for type B is provided in Example 4.2, Example 4.3, and its calculation is explained in Appendix A. In section 5, we explain another kind of quantum tori, that is, the quantum tori arising from the quantum cluster algebra structures of quantized coordinate algebras. In section 6, we prove the isomorphism between these two kinds of quantum tori as explained just after (1.2). In section 7, we recall the definition of the quantum Grothendieck rings and the 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters of standard modules and simple modules. In section 8, we briefly recall the basics of quantized coordinate algebras including (normalized) unipotent quantum minors, quantum determinantal identities, (normalized) dual Poincaré-Birkhoff-Witt type bases and dual canonical bases. In section 9, we prove the quantum T-system for type 
B
n
(
1
)
 relying on a feature of type 
B
n
(
1
)
: the thinness of Kirillov-Reshetikhin modules. In section 10, we prove the isomorphism (1.2) together with the correspondence between 
(
q
,
t
)
-characters of simple modules and dual canonical basis elements. In section 11, we give the proof of the positivities (P1)–(P3) and explain the coincidence between our isomorphism and the isomorphism established by Kashiwara and Oh [43], which implies (1.1) in 
C
Q
,
B
n
. In section 12, we provide the explicit correspondence of simple modules under (1.4) for some explicit quivers.

3. The monoidal subcategories associated with quivers
In [30], the first author and Leclerc introduced specific monoidal subcategories 
C
Q
 of 
C
•
 in the case that the quantum loop algebra is of type 
X

(
1
)
 with 
X
=
A
,
D
 or E. These subcategories are constructed from a Dynkin quiver 
Q
 of type 
X
N
. In particular, the essential datum is the Auslander-Reiten quiver 
Γ
Q
 associated with 
Q
. It is known that the subcategory 
C
Q
 has interesting properties. For example, its complexified Grothendieck ring is isomorphic to the coordinate algebra 
C
[
N
−
]
 of the unipotent group 
N
−
 of type 
X
N
, and the simple modules in 
C
Q
 categorify the dual canonical basis of 
C
[
N
−
]
 (see Theorem 12.1). Moreover it is a “heart” of the whole category (cf. [30, subsection 1.5]). See subsection 3.2 for the definition of 
C
Q
, and see subsection 12.1 and [30], [16] for detailed properties.
In this paper, we deal also with an analogue of these subcategories in the case of type 
B
n
(
1
)
, which is introduced by Oh and Suh [57]. To define these subcategories, we need combinatorially generalized Auslander-Reiten quivers, called the twisted Auslander-Reiten quivers [56], [57]. We emphasize that the combinatorial generalization of Auslander-Reiten quivers of type 
A
2
n
−
1
 are used in order to define the subcategories of 
C
•
 of type 
B
n
(
1
)
.
3.1. The adapted reduced words and Auslander-Reiten quivers
In this subsection, we assume that

=

,

. A vertex

Let i be a sink or a source of

(

)

3.3. Combinatorial Auslander-Reiten quivers
The Auslander-Reiten quiver

We consider the general setting in subsection 2.1 unless otherwise specified.

4. Quantum tori
In the following, we deal with a t-deformed q-character homomorphism, called a 
(
q
,
t
)
-character. First, we define a t-deformed version of 
Y
=
Z
[
Y
i
,
r
±
1
|
i
∈
I
,
r
∈
Z
]
 following [23]. The definition in [23] comes from the original construction of q-characters in [15]: the variables 
Y
i
,
p
 are defined as formal power series that pairwise commute. In [23], these formal power series are replaced by certain infinite sums which can be seen as vertex operators and give rise to the t-commutative variables (the parameter t itself appears as a formal power series). In [54], [60], a slightly different quantum torus is defined for the simply-laced case from a convolution operation for certain perverse sheaves on quiver varieties.

5. Quantum tori for quantized coordinate algebras
We prepare the other quantum tori which arise from quantum cluster algebra structures of quantized coordinate algebras [4]. Quantum cluster algebras are specific subalgebras of skew field of fractions (and, a posteriori, subalgebras of quantum tori) with infinitely many generators in general. It is known that many quantized coordinate algebras in Lie theory have quantum cluster algebra structures. See [19], [20], [21] and references therein.
In this section, we only recall the definitions concerning these quantum tori, and review the quantum tori associated with quantum cluster algebra structures of some quantized coordinate algebras. See Appendix B for the complete definition of quantum cluster algebras.

Remark 8.1

There are several conventions of the definitions of quantized coordinate algebras and (normalized) unipotent quantum minors. For example, in [30], quantized coordinate algebras are defined through the positive half of the quantized enveloping algebras. 11. Corollaries of the isomorphism

˜

♭
. In subsection 11.2, we provide an example of such calculation.

Remark 12.10

The correspondence given by

˜
